ParsiComBat ranks candidate acquisition parameters by batch effect, filters redundancy, and scores sequential ComBat corrections. We evaluated it in three experiments: in simulations, testing whether it recovered a known batch variable and abstained without one; in CT (NSCLC-Radiomics) and MRI (MAMA-MIA) cohorts, comparing batch-effect removal against manufacturer (manual), every feasible parameter (greedy) and AutoCombat; and in clinical prediction, internally and externally. In simulations, ParsiComBat identified the true batch variable in 92.9% of positive trials and abstained in 99.2% of null controls. In CT, correcting for reconstruction kernel reduced mean k-nearest neighbour batch-effect test from 0.276 to 0.032, almost matching greedy correction of six variables. In MRI, correcting for slice thickness and manufacturer reduced it from 0.680 to 0.325, slightly more than manual selection but less than greedy correction of seven variables. Removing technical variation did not improve clinical discrimination: no strategy, including raw features, differed significantly from ParsiComBat in area under the curve. ParsiComBat provides a data-driven framework that removes technical batch effects using minimal acquisition parameters, preserving feature structure and clinical discrimination. It selects variables verifiable against the acquisition protocol and abstains when no correction is needed, supporting transparent, reproducible multicentre radiomics.

### 104. [Conditional Independence Graph Learning from Dependent Data Via Time-Delay Embedding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34616v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：TIME series, conditional independence graph, graph estimation, dependent data via time-delay, non-convex penalties and graph, long-range dependent LRD
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34616v1>

**原始摘要**

We consider the problem of inferring the conditional independence graph (CIG) of a high-dimensional stationary multivariate Gaussian time series which could be short-range dependent (SRD) or long-range dependent (LRD). In a time series graph, each component of the vector series is represented by a distinct node, and associations between components are represented by edges between the corresponding nodes. We consider a time-domain approach based on time-delay embedding, casting the problem as one of multi-attribute graph estimation for random vectors where a vector is associated with each node of the graph. For an embedding dimension $d$, at each node the associated $(d+1)$-dimensional random vector consists of a time series component and its $d$ delayed copies, with integer delays up to $d \ge 1$. Graph estimation from dependent data has been investigated in the past using frequency-domain methods whose analysis is restricted to SRD data. We consider a penalized negative pseudo log-likelihood function with convex lasso or non-convex regularizations to estimate the CIG. Non-convex regularization functions are log-sum, smoothly clipped absolute deviation (SCAD) and minimax concave penalty (MCP). We exploit an existing alternating direction method of multipliers (ADMM) approach to optimize the objective function. We establish sufficient conditions in a high-dimensional setting for consistency, local convexity when using non-convex penalties, and graph recovery, for both SRD and LRD series. We illustrate our approach with both synthetic and real-data examples. For a $p$-dimensional time series, the computational complexity of the ADMM algorithm is ${\cal O}((d+1)^3 p^3)$. Based on the numerical examples, a heuristic is suggested and illustrated for selection of $d$ which, in theory, should tend to infinity to infer the time series graph.

### 105. [Commit2Drive: Perception-Grounded Language Decisions as Commitments for Trajectory Planning in Vision-Language-Action Driving Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34614v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：commit2drive, language decision, decision and the trajectory, vision-language, reasoning, trajectory together and rule-based
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34614v1>

**原始摘要**

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models introduce language reasoning into end-to-end autonomous driving. They output a language decision together with a trajectory, which makes the planner more interpretable. However, in existing methods the language decision is either an independent output or an index that selects among similar candidate trajectories, and its annotations are not verified against the trajectory. As a result, the language decision and the trajectory are often inconsistent in closed-loop driving. To address this, we propose Commit2Drive, a framework that imposes the language decision as a consistency constraint on trajectory planning. The decision is a commitment, a maneuver class with a continuous parameter that one deterministic rule reads from any trajectory, and it is grounded in facts reported by the perception of the model itself. A Maneuver Bank maps the commitment to a code with a kinematic meaning, and consistency losses train the planner to execute every admissible decision of a scene, so that the trajectory follows the decision. An automatic data pipeline supplies the supervision. Decisions are labeled from the future motion of the expert, counterfactual executions train the decision and the trajectory together, and rule-based functions with a Vision-Language Model (VLM) generate, review and refine the language annotations. The code is a lookup that involves no hidden state of the language model, so Commit2Drive is extensible to rule-based decisions, which the same planner executes without retraining. Commit2Drive uses a 0.5B language model and runs closed-loop inference online on a single consumer graphics card. On Bench2Drive, Commit2Drive reaches a Driving Score (DS) of 78.50 and a Success Rate (SR) of 55.91% with a lightweight model, which is competitive with the state of the art, and greatly reduces the language-action inconsistency. Code will be released soon.

### 106. [Do We Really Need an LLM for Weight-Space Programming? A Deterministic Forward-Only Paradigm and Its Capability Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34612v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：weight-space programming, reasoning, MoE, FULL gsm8k and math-500., bridges forward-only and gradient, forward-only and gradient adaptation.
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34612v1>

**原始摘要**

Recent work on Weight-Space Programming uses LLMs to edit neural weights with forward passes only, conflating LLM scripting with the underlying algebra of forward-only editing. We show that for core structured editing regimes, an LLM optimizer is unnecessary, and introduce Forward Weight-Space Programming (FWSP): a deterministic pipeline combining probing, localization, stratified operators (O1-O4), automatic blind routing (83.3% vs. 33.3% for a 27B LLM with the same tool library), and paired verification with zero additional per-token computation. For reasoning LLMs, we propose Protected FisherGate, a closed-form covariance-whitened readout edit orthogonal to normal-reasoning states: on DeepSeek-R1-0528-8B, a single frozen math-calibrated patch achieves 84.2% HumanEval pass@1 (1K budget; 75.4 +/- 9.4% across 3/5 Stage-4-passing subsets), beating 512-token-reserved budget forcing by +14.6 pp (-23.4% tokens) while transferring across HF-to-vLLM backends on full GSM8K and MATH-500. Across five modalities and thirteen models (50K to 27B dense and FP8 MoE), we further prove and verify two operator boundaries, subspace entanglement and frozen representation span, where FWSP-localized LoRA bridges forward-only and gradient adaptation.

## 具身智能 / 强化学习

### 1. [MidasTUI: Instant Tangible User Interfaces for Wearable Displays using Everyday Objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34945v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：tangible USER interfaces, everyday objects, wearable displays, transformer, object using the wearable, displays can bring digital
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34945v1>

**原始摘要**

While wearable displays can bring digital workspaces anywhere, interaction remains limited by mid-air gestures that lack tactile feedback and induce fatigue, or controllers that are cumbersome to carry. We present MidasTUI, a system that instantly transforms arbitrary everyday objects into opportunistic tangible user interfaces through brief interaction demonstrations. For example, a user can slide an earbud case across a table as a mouse, enabling opportunistic interaction without carrying a dedicated input device. MidasTUI detects when a user is holding and moving an object using the wearable display camera and a Transformer-based temporal classifier. It then infers the intended input modality (e.g., slider, mouse, keypad) from hand movement and maps the tracked hand-object interaction to an application function. Our evaluations demonstrate reliable cross-object and cross-user generalization. A comparative user study further shows that grounding interaction in everyday objects reduces physical burden and improves perceived usability and control relative to mid-air interaction.

### 2. [AnyCam: Anything Can Represent Camera for Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34944v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：video generation, camera-controlled video, diffusion, generator for on-the-fly camera, on-the-fly camera control. BOTH, few-step generator for on-the-fly
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34944v1>

**原始摘要**

Camera-controlled video generation re-renders a video along a target camera trajectory, yet moving the camera around an object, the most natural way to change the viewpoint of an object-centric scene, remains difficult. Existing methods condition either on camera parameters, which are scale-ambiguous in monocular video, or on explicit geometry such as warped point clouds or depth, which breaks down under large viewpoint changes. We present AnyCam, a method that represents the camera with an anchor object in the scene: we reconstruct it in 3D and render it along the target trajectory, a camera signal that is scale-aware, view-complete and easy to specify relative to the object. A video diffusion model transfers the content and dynamics of the source video to the new viewpoint. Its H-Concat layout aligns source and target frames in time, so the model can be distilled into AnyCam-AR, a streaming few-step generator for on-the-fly camera control. Both models are trained on paired videos with pixel-aligned anchor renderings from multi-camera videos and static-scene captures. Experiments on diverse datasets and trajectories show that AnyCam performs favorably against camera-controlled video generation and 3D reconstruction methods in camera control and visual quality.

### 3. [Tracking Any Point with Agentic Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34943v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：agent, reasoning, fine-tuning, vision-language, predictions. with gpt-6 astra, point identity and refine
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34943v1>

**原始摘要**

A general-purpose Vision-Language Model (VLM) has surpassed expert vision models in many domains such as object detection and video recognition. However, point correspondence is known to be challenging for VLMs since it goes beyond object-level recognition: it requires pixel-level localization of the same physical surface point across observations, even when appearance changes or the point disappears and later reappears. In this paper, we introduce AgenTAP, a VLM-based agent that directly predicts point coordinates and visibility and actively reasons over videos. Starting from its own estimate, the agent autonomously chooses what to inspect and uses the resulting evidence to verify point identity and refine its predictions. With GPT-6 Astra, it surpasses expert vision models in both point localization and visibility prediction, without correspondence-specific fine-tuning. We further use AgenTAP to re-identify physical points after disappearance and reconnect fragmented tracks, constructing EgoTAP-4D, a dataset of 3D point trajectories in long real-world egocentric videos. On sampled reappearing points, its predictions approach human agreement, and adding EgoTAP-4D to D4RT training improves average 3D tracking performance.

### 4. [Spectrum Adaptation of AI-Native Autoencoder Communications with Transfer Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34942v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：fading with practical front-end, ai-native communications with AES, physical-layer learning replaces separate, end-to-end physical-layer learning replaces, learning. end-to-end physical-layer learning, transfer learning. end-to-end physical-layer
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34942v1>

**原始摘要**

End-to-end physical-layer learning replaces separate modulation, coding, and detection operations with an autoencoder (AE) that consists of a jointly trained encoder-decoder pair at the transmitter and the receiver. This paper studies AI-native communications with AEs and quantifies the sensitivity to training-test mismatch in channel models. Models trained only on an AWGN channel achieve low bit error rates (BERs) under matched conditions but degrade severely on Rayleigh fading with practical front-end impairments. To reduce the time and energy required to adapt to new channel conditions, we propose a low-latency transfer-learning procedure that starts from an AWGN-pretrained system and fine-tunes only a small subset of transmitter and receiver layers while freezing the remainder. We further formulate layer selection as a budget-constrained optimization problem that identifies the most informative parameters to update during adaptation. This preserves the learned signal geometry and the deep decision structure while adapting lightweight front ends to fading- and impairment-induced mixing. For AE communications, we show that partial updates remove the mismatch-induced error floor and approach the BER of fully retrained models. We provide a layer-wise study and quantify resource-performance trade-offs, showing that modest adaptation substantially reduces BER while cutting training time, multiply-accumulate (MAC) operations, energy consumption, and the fraction of updated parameters for resource-efficient implementation.

### 5. [LuxAvatar: Real-Time Relightable Head Avatars via Causal Neural Shading of 3D Intrinsic Proxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34940v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：causal autoregressive neural, neural shader, diffusion, fine-tuning, distillation, framework for real-time HEAD
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34940v1>

**原始摘要**

We present LuxAvatar, a feed-forward framework for real-time head avatar animation and relighting from a single portrait. To achieve both 3D controllability and complex facial light transport, we factorize avatar synthesis into an animatable 3D intrinsic Gaussian proxy and a causal autoregressive neural shader. This proxy disentangles canonical geometry and reflectance attributes, rapidly rasterizing deformed Gaussians into view- and motion-consistent PBR buffers. Conditioned on these buffers, our neural shader formulates forward rendering as a conditional video diffusion process, bypassing explicit PBR computation to synthesize realistic shading effects under target illumination. We achieve real-time streaming through heterogeneous distillation from a pretrained multi-step bidirectional relighting teacher to a two-step causal autoregressive neural shader, inheriting broad synthetic lighting priors while minimizing conditioning and decoding latency. To further specialize these priors to human heads, we construct a hybrid relighting dataset for domain-specific fine-tuning, capturing authentic facial dynamics and physically grounded light transport. Extensive experiments show that our framework enables 3D-controllable, real-time portrait animation and relighting, outperforming state-of-the-art methods in visual fidelity, motion control, and lighting accuracy.

### 6. [A Mesh for Every Moment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34939v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：MESH for every, benchmark, dynamic gaussian splatting methods, every frame. existing dynamic, keeps every gaussian, every moment
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34939v1>

**原始摘要**

We present MEMO (a Mesh for Every MOment), a method that reconstructs the geometry of complete dynamic scenes, captured by multiple synchronized cameras, and extracts a mesh at every frame. Existing dynamic Gaussian splatting methods either model appearance alone or target isolated objects segmented by foreground masks, and all of them recover meshes by TSDF fusion or Poisson reconstruction, whose fixed grids limit the detail they can capture. At the core of MEMO is a per-Gaussian motion model inspired by Gaussian processes, which represents each Gaussian's position and rotation over time as a combination of temporal Gaussian kernels. The model keeps every Gaussian a valid surface element throughout the sequence and carries an implicit representation of the dynamic geometry, so a mesh can be derived at any time from a field defined by the Gaussians. Although common in real captures, large and fast motion poses a challenge that dynamic Gaussian splatting methods usually overlook. To account for it, we design a track-based initialization scheme that fits each Gaussian's initial trajectory to point tracks in closed form. We evaluate MEMO on Blender-Moments, a new benchmark of nine animated scenes with ground-truth meshes at every frame, and on two real-world datasets. MEMO reconstructs the most accurate geometry among state-of-the-art methods, and remains competitive in novel-view synthesis and training time. Project page: https://memo-4d.github.io

### 7. [DexLink: Synthesizing Dexterous Behaviors from Chained Hand-object Poses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34936v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：synthesizing diverse dexterous, reinforcement learning, dexterous behaviors, control OVER the hand-object, behaviors from chained hand-object, OVER the hand-object interactions.
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34936v1>

**原始摘要**

Synthesizing diverse dexterous manipulation policies is challenging: existing simulation-based approaches rely on human demonstrations which are difficult to scale, or use generic reward functions, which provide limited control over the hand-object interactions. We propose DexLink, which addresses these limitations by synthesizing diverse dexterous behaviors from sparse, reusable hand-object interaction templates. Given key task waypoints, DexLink retargets static interaction templates to target objects using an optimization that preserves spatial hand-object relationships. It then links the retargeted poses into an approximate reference trajectory and uses residual reinforcement learning to refine this reference into a closed-loop policy in simulation. Experiments spanning single-hand, bimanual, articulated, and dynamic tasks demonstrate that DexLink provides fine-grained control over task timing, grasp types, and high-level execution. The modular interaction templates transfer reliably across object scales, instances, and categories; without object-specific human demonstrations, the framework also matches the performance of dense reference-tracking methods. Finally, zero-shot deployment of policies trained purely in simulation shows promise for the simulation-based framework.

### 8. [Can image generation help visual reasoning? An empirical study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34935v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：reasoning, multimodal, vision-language, repeated image edits yield, image generation HELP visual, pairs generation lowers MEAN
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34935v1>

**原始摘要**

Language models think in words; then multimodal models might think in images. We test whether a generated helper improves the answer to a visual question. A model describes the helper it needs and then answers using the original and generated images together. Across 13 vision-language model and generator pairs, generation lowers mean accuracy by 3.7 percentage points, with declines on nine of ten tasks. Two unified models, BAGEL and Janus-Pro-7B, each lose accuracy on eight tasks. On VSTAR, adding a generated image to the same planning hint lowers accuracy by 10.47 points. Repeated image edits yield different outcomes across tasks. ChartQAPro and MathVista stay below their no-generation baselines, while ERQA gains three points after ten edits.

### 9. [Attack Policy Selection can Mislead Monitor Evaluators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34934v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：attack policies, reinforcement learning, attack policy, reasoning, dangerous behaviour, ex-production openai monitor
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34934v1>

**原始摘要**

AI monitors, classifiers that flag dangerous behaviour by AI agents, are often evaluated against transcripts generated by attack policies acting as proxies for dangerous behaviour in deployment. We evaluate an ex-production OpenAI monitor as a case study and find that measured false negative rate is highly sensitive to both the method used to generate attack policies and the level of optimisation applied within that method. This rate ranged from 0.5\% to 20.8\% across optimisation levels of a single method (reinforcement learning), and from 6.4\% to 23.0\% across methods at their best observed optimisation level (optimised prompting vs.\ transcript editing). Every attack policy was developed against proxy monitors only: all reported results show attacks transferring to the held-out GPT-5.4 target monitor. Our results suggest that monitor evasion evaluations can easily mislead: an evaluation using only basic attack methods may give false confidence that a monitor is strong. The sensitivity to attack-policy choice raises a problem: interpreting any particular result as evidence about deployment performance requires justification. The transcripts produced by attack policies are proxies for the dangerous behaviour a monitor must flag in deployment. These proxies can introduce visible artefacts, such as reasoning about a ``side task'' absent from the instructions shown to the monitor, that affect monitor judgements. Given the sensitivity above, the choice of proxy can change an evaluation's conclusion. Reading these results as evidence about deployment therefore requires a separate legitimacy argument for why the proxies are informative, including any justification of conservativeness. Making convincing arguments remains an open problem.

### 10. [MERLoc: Robust Robot Localization in Dynamic Environments with Pose-Based Retrieval over 3D Gaussian Splatting References](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34932v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：pose-based retrieval, gaussian splatting, REAL dynamic scenes pose-based, dynamic environments with pose-based, space for the robot, views are sampled OVER
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34932v1>

**原始摘要**

Robots operating in previously mapped spaces must localize accurately despite moving people and rearranged objects. Most localization pipelines match the query against a sparse set of captured reference images retrieved by appearance, which is brittle under scene change and repeating structures. We present MERLoc, which turns a single metric 3D Gaussian Splatting scan into a dense Functional Reference Memory. Reference views are sampled over the navigable space for the robot's morphology and sensors at a target density and rendered offline. Pre-encoding them for the chosen off-the-shelf localizer removes the reference encoder from inference. A pose-based retrieval strategy then selects references by spatial proximity and viewing direction rather than appearance, sidestepping visual ambiguities whenever a prior exists. Across one classical and four learned localizers in simulated and real dynamic scenes, pose-based retrieval matches or outperforms appearance retrieval in most cases, even when the latter is gated by the same prior. On real data, it lowers position RMSE by 15-60 % in nine of ten backend/split combinations, and the rendered memory outperforms real-image caches posed by Structure-from-Motion. The system runs onboard a Jetson Orin NX at 5 Hz, enabling a navigation policy to successfully traverse a hallway with people roaming and rearranging objects.

### 11. [One Ocean, Many Tasks: A Data-Grounded Simulation Suite and Benchmark for Marine Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34925v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：data-grounded simulation suite, simulation suite and benchmark, agent, data-driven currents and tides, disturbances and reveal trade-offs, navigation and station-keeping under
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34925v1>

**原始摘要**

Simulation is essential for marine robotics, yet existing platforms often trade off between visually realistic worlds, high-throughput experimentation, and ocean-condition realism grounded in real data. We present OneOcean, a data-grounded simulation suite and benchmark built around a unified spatiotemporal ocean-environment product that harmonizes bathymetry with data-driven currents and tides, with optional pollution fields for cleanup-related evaluation. On top of this environment representation, OneOcean defines a task ladder spanning navigation and station-keeping under currents, waypoint and route following, depth-profile tracking, area scanning, pipeline inspection, and multi-agent coordination, including formation transit, fish protection and patrol (herding), and surface pollution localization, containment, and cleanup. The suite supports multi-agent settings with 2-10 vehicles. Our simulator supports three to six Degrees of Freedom (DoF) and produces standardized metrics and reproducible run manifests, enabling consistent aggregation across tasks, difficulties, and scaling settings. Experiments across dataset variants and scenes systematically stress planning and control under data-grounded disturbances and reveal trade-offs among heuristic baselines, Behavior Cloning (BC), and a Large Language Model (LLM)-based planner.

### 12. [$μ$-Bench: Benchmarking Multi-User Task Completion in Shared Interaction Voice Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34920v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：agent, joint task success, benchmark, multi-user requests while upholding, multi-user simulation with verifiable, session-level outcomes bench provides
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34920v1>

**原始摘要**

Voice agents in shared spaces must satisfy interdependent multi-user requests while upholding per-user authority, privacy, and task constraints. Existing evaluations offer limited coverage when participants react throughout a session. We introduce $μ$-Bench, a benchmark for joint task completion in closed-loop, full-duplex multi-user voice interaction. Simulated participants act from isolated profiles of goals, knowledge, and permissions, adapting to the agent's speech and tool outcomes. Across 20 domains, 100 scenarios vary group size, acoustics, and follow-up demands to form 800 cases (408 tool-augmented, 392 tool-free). Joint Task Success (JTS) requires all user goals, privacy boundaries, and terminal states to hold jointly, while Pure JTS further disallows unauthorized tool calls; Attribution, Threading, and Coordination (ATC) diagnostics localize failures. Across 13 voice agents, proprietary systems peak at 62.24\% tool-free and 65.44\% tool-augmented success, while open-weight models remain below 20\%. ATC diagnostics show that open-weight models lag primarily in multi-turn attribution and threading, and controlled contrasts reveal that larger groups consistently degrade joint task success across capable systems. By coupling reactive multi-user simulation with verifiable session-level outcomes, $μ$-Bench provides a rigorous testbed for shared voice agents.

### 13. [Memory Is Not Ground Truth: Revising Navigation Authority under Unreliable VLM Evidence for Persistent Semantic Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34915v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：reasoning, vision-language, remain ambiguous under vlm-based, about semantic and spatio-temporal, semantic and spatio-temporal context, ambiguous under vlm-based perception.
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34915v1>

**原始摘要**

Vision-language models (VLMs) enable robots to reason about semantic and spatio-temporal context, verify task-relevant objects, and guide sensing and navigation. Persistent memory can reduce redundant search for field robots, but when should a memory continue to guide behavior? Repeated misses may suggest that a memory is stale. Our experiments show why this evidence can remain ambiguous under VLM-based perception. Using a Boston Dynamics Spot with four fixed cameras and a persistent spatio-temporal visual memory, we measure negative-verification behavior across targets, viewpoints, language queries, and repeated revisits. False-negative rates vary substantially across objects, nearby revisits often reproduce the same detector outcome, and some matched target-present and target-removed experiments yield identical miss histories. This ambiguity persists under changed observation geometry, identity-preserving query reformulation, and conditioning on a previously verified target image. These findings raise two questions: is the object still there, and is this memory still useful for navigation? We introduce a dependence-aware, reversible memory-update mechanism that avoids double-counting correlated misses and stops using unsupported memories as direct navigation goals without claiming that the object is absent. Later positive evidence can restore a memory's use for navigation. One resident onboard VLM is shared across memory verification, semantic frontier reasoning, and in-motion perception. In three matched real-robot decision contexts, an episode-local baseline selected a stale memory as its navigation objective in every case, whereas our method selected semantic frontiers in all three. A complementary trial re-verified the target during motion and promoted fresh memory. These results support revising memory actionability rather than interpreting failed detection as object absence.

### 14. [Cairn: Object-Centric 3D Trajectory Priors for Steering Frozen VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34914v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：benchmark, fine-tuning, vision-language, VLAS are capable low-level, keeps the VLA frozen, VLA frozen and steers
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34914v1>

**原始摘要**

Vision-language-action models (VLAs) are capable low-level controllers, but they tie where objects go to the layouts they were trained on: when objects are moved or obstacles block the way, they carry them to the wrong place or into collisions. Fine-tuning on new layouts needs robot data for each one and erodes skills the model already has. We introduce Cairn, which keeps the VLA frozen and steers it at test time along learned 3D trajectories of the object in its gripper. A small prior reads point clouds of the scene and the held object, along with its pose when grasped and the instruction, and samples SE(3) trajectories up to its release that lead it around obstacles and turn it to fit through gaps. The prior and the VLA form the two levels of a hierarchical planner in which feedback runs both ways: the VLA's intended motion chooses and reshapes the trajectories, and the chosen one guides the VLA's denoising. Its predicted actions, mapped to the held object's poses through the grasp, are pulled toward the next waypoint, while the VLA's weights stay untouched. Across 1,941 held-out LIBERO scenes with displaced objects and obstacles, Cairn raises the success rate of a frozen π0.5 from 24.5% to 95.1%, above that of a π0.5 fine-tuned on our training layouts, without hurting standard LIBERO. On the third-party LIBERO-PRO benchmark, it raises success from 27.3% to 38.0%, and from 54.4% to 81.1% on the episodes it steers. On a real Franka, with a prior trained purely in simulation, it guides the frozen DROID π0.5 around an obstacle and to a placement at the limit of the arm's reach, where it otherwise fails. All code, models and data will be made public.

### 15. [Agentic LongLive: Asynchronous Agentic Memory for Long Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34907v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：agentic longlive, memory for LONG video, agentic memory for LONG, LONG video generation., asynchronous agentic memory, reinforcement learning
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34907v1>

**原始摘要**

Real-time autoregressive video generators stream minutes of video and accept new prompts on the fly, but struggle to keep people, objects, and places consistent over a long interactive story. Agent systems provide such memory for offline multi-shot generation, yet they read the whole script in advance, plan each shot before generating it, and regenerate failed shots, which a live stream does not allow. We present Agentic LongLive, an asynchronous agentic memory for long video generation. A memory agent on separate GPUs reads only the prompts revealed so far, never the generated video, and keeps a text state and evolving images for every person, object, and scene. From this entity memory it composes a global image and a per-shot memory image, which an auxiliary GPU prefills into the generator's attention sink before the next shot starts, and writes entity cards for the prompts that name them, so the video never waits for memory and video errors are not written back. A small language model, trained with reinforcement learning and an image reward calibrated using downstream video outcomes, decides what to remember before the next prompt is known. On LongStory-Online, a new benchmark of 25 stories (857 shots) revealed one shot at a time, Agentic LongLive improves persistent-state accuracy of LongLive-2.0, its base generator, by 26.3 points, protagonist identity by 44.9, and revisited-place consistency by 40.4, at about 1% extra generation time on H100. The full system streams faster than real time on one 8xB200 node with the generator in BF16 (34.3 FPS), keeps image quality stable over 14-minute stories, and improves identity on two MiniMax-H3 generators, one of which, LongLive-H3, also runs in real time with it. To our knowledge, it is the first real-time video generator with text and image memory kept by an LLM agent.

### 16. [What a Drop Reveals: Sim-to-Real Material Estimation for Deformable Objects from Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34894v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：DROP reveals sim-to-real material, reveals sim-to-real material estimation, physreader-real everyday objects dropped, introduce physreader-real everyday objects, better READ from WHAT, domains THAN from WHAT
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34894v1>

**原始摘要**

How an object deforms when it hits the ground reveals what it is made of. From a single real video of a drop, we estimate a deformable object's density-normalized stiffness $E/ρ$, and its yield stress $σ_Y/ρ$ when the impact causes plastic deformation, with a feed-forward predictor trained only in simulation. Real videos carry no material labels, so such predictors are developed and validated on synthetic data alone. We show that synthetic validation ranks their geometric inputs in the wrong order. Material acts through 3D deformation, and tracklets lifted to 3D with estimated depth are indeed the most accurate input in simulation. However, on real drops they are the least accurate, and better depth does not repair them: the failure persists with simulator depth in training, LiDAR depth at test time, and exact depth on rendered replays of the real drops. Short 2D optical-flow tracklets do the opposite: they improve nothing in simulation, yet they transfer best. Material is therefore better read from what perception measures consistently in both domains than from what is most informative in simulation. Our predictor, PhysReader, reads the video and 2D tracklets and learns the lift to 3D in simulation by matching a teacher that sees exact 3D deformation. To measure transfer, we introduce PhysReader-Real: 16 everyday objects dropped five times each, with reference materials fit jointly to each object's drops and checked against tensile tests. On PhysReader-Real, PhysReader is closer to the reference materials than every other input and baseline. The one exception is a per-scene optimizer started from PhysReader's prediction, which ends slightly closer but takes 49 minutes per video instead of 12 seconds.

### 17. [FineDex: Bridging Fine-Grained Human-Object Interaction Generation to Dexterous Humanoid Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34884v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：human-object interaction, fine-grained instructions, HOI generation, dexterous humanoid, dexterous manipulation, agent
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34884v1>

**原始摘要**

Dexterity is, at its core, finger-level control, yet existing dexterous manipulation and human-object interaction (HOI) generation methods rarely let one specify how an interaction is performed: five-fingered hands are often reduced to grippers, or finger usage is copied from individual human demonstrations. We present FineDex, a framework that makes finger-level intent both expressible and executable. FineDex learns coordinated human body, hand, and object motions that follow fine-grained instructions on hand selection, individual fingers, and contacts with articulated objects. To bridge generation and execution, FineDex leverages generated interactions as actionable priors for an agent that discovers physically feasible contact strategies through practice in a physics-based digital twin. By adapting at test time without per-instance retraining, FineDex generalizes across fine-grained instructions and object configurations. Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art HOI generation quality and fine-grained controllability, substantial improvements in dexterous manipulation success, and successful deployment on a real dexterous humanoid robot.

### 18. [Stranger Things: When Objects Appear Without Their Typical Neighbors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34881v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：WHEN objects appear, benchmark, multimodal, transformer, vision-language, large language models
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34881v1>

**原始摘要**

Visual context is a powerful cue for object recognition: scenes inform what, where, and at what scale objects are likely to appear. Yet over-reliance on context becomes a failure mode when models predict objects based on their typical neighbors rather than the objects' own visual features, which we call typicality bias. While extensively studied in the pre-Transformer era, it has often been assumed that architectural and scale improvements would naturally solve it, and a systematic diagnostic across the modern vision stack remains absent. We provide such a diagnostic framework. We measure typicality bias via normalized pointwise mutual information (NPMI) over object pairs, and introduce STRANGE-Bench (Synthetic Typicality-RANked Grounded Evaluation Benchmark): a photorealistic, layout-controlled benchmark spanning 17 sub-tiers from typical to never-co-occurring object pairs. Across five model families (specialist detectors, open-vocabulary detectors, self-supervised, vision-language, and multimodal large language models) and three recognition tasks (supervised detection, open-vocabulary detection, and classification), the bias is prevalent: gaps of 5-11 p.p. persist when objects appear outside their typical company, and do not consistently shrink with scale. To mitigate it, we turn to data augmentation: existing augmentations are designed for visual diversity, not for breaking co-occurrence shortcuts, and sample from the very distribution that produces typicality bias. We propose Anchor-Atypicality Sampling (AAS), a simple sampling rule for paste-based augmentations that uses the NPMI signal to tilt the training-time co-occurrence distribution toward atypical partners. AAS reduces the typicality gap by up to 1.6 p.p. on natural-image COCO splits and 23.0 p.p. on synthetic STRANGE-Bench across three model families, without sacrificing overall performance.

### 19. [Towards Closing the Performance Gap in Cross-Modal Place Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34877v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：place recognition, cross-modal place, attention, fine-tuning, text-to-image, vision-language
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34877v1>

**原始摘要**

Cross-modal place recognition (CMPR) matches natural language descriptions against databases of geo-tagged reference images, enabling localization without visual queries. Despite its potential for language-guided navigation, existing vision-language models lag far behind visual place recognition (VPR) baselines, even after task adaptation. Unlike general-purpose text-to-image retrieval, place recognition relies on subtle local cues to distinguish visually similar locations. We therefore hypothesize that a primary bottleneck in CMPR is capturing fine-grained correspondences between image regions and text details, which requires higher visual token granularity, strong text representation capacity, and local cross-modal matching. To address this challenge, we propose CrossPlace, a two-stage CMPR framework. Stage 1 performs global retrieval by pairing a jointly aligned DINOv2-B/14 visual backbone with a progressively fine-tuned BGE-Large text encoder, while Stage 2 refines top candidates using a lightweight token-level cross-attention reranker. Through systematic analysis, we isolate the drivers of retrieval accuracy and show that visual token density provides the largest performance improvement, while text encoder capacity and task fine-tuning offer complementary gains. To evaluate robustness under realistic conditions, we also extend CMPR evaluation to city-scale and severe weather and illumination settings. Across all existing and new benchmarks, CrossPlace achieves double-digit Recall@1 improvements over prior cross-modal methods, closes the gap to unimodal text retrieval, and narrows it to unimodal VPR. Code and benchmarks are available at https://github.com/oferidan1/CrossPlace

### 20. [RePace: Reasoning about Demonstration Speedup for Faster Vision-Language-Action Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34864v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：VLA policies, speedup factor, reasoning, vision-language, alignment, fine-tuning
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34864v1>

**原始摘要**

Since vision-language-action (VLA) policies can inherit the slow pace of human demonstrations, accelerating these demonstrations before fine-tuning can lead to faster VLA policies without an additional speed-selection module at deployment. Several offline speedup methods have focused on identifying segments that require less precision and assigning predefined speedup factors to the segments. However, even within a single segment, a speedup factor suitable for steady motion may be too high when precise alignment is required. To this end, this paper presents RePace, an offline reasoning framework that determines how much to accelerate each part of a demonstration. For the same recorded movement, RePace measures how much each candidate speedup factor shortens the sequence and compares the resulting observation-action pairs used for training. Given the task instruction, a pretrained language model uses the visual context and original motion of successive windows to interpret these candidate retiming effects and select a speedup profile for the complete episode. Experimental results demonstrate that RePace outperforms the baselines in overall task execution efficiency on both LIBERO and RoboCasa-Kitchen. On all three tasks with a gripper-equipped Franka Research 3 arm, RePace matches or exceeds the acceleration baselines in success rate and completes successful trials faster.

### 21. [Focus Only on What's Important: Training-Free Focus Maps for Frozen Vision Transformers in Long-Video Object Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34863v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：protecting ONLY the previous, training-free focus, vision transformers, object segmentation., frozen dinov3 VIT inside, training-free token merging CUTS
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34863v1>

**原始摘要**

Vision transformers are strong but costly image encoders for video object segmentation. Training-free token merging cuts compute uniformly across the frame, including on tracked objects. We give a frozen DINOv3 ViT inside a trained Cutie head a training-free focus map: full resolution where the segmenter expects its objects and coarser merging elsewhere. Protecting only the previous frame's mask works on short clips but fails on long videos, where fast motion pushes targets outside the margin and into background merging, locking in loss. Our policy instead takes the union of velocity-shifted and block-matched masks, runs a camera-compensated surprise check, delegates uncertain regions to the compared merger, and falls back to it when all objects are lost. On long videos (LVOS V2 valid, pre-specified protocol, three ViT-S seeds), it beats ALGM 0.98 by +2.76 J&F (95% object CI [+1.22,+4.44]; video-cluster [+0.99,+4.52]) at 16% fewer counted encoder FLOPs (0.385 vs. 0.459), sitting 1.0 J&F point below the dense-encoder baseline; at ViT-B the gain over ALGM 0.95 is +3.89 [+2.03,+5.87]. On DAVIS 2017 val it stays within -0.19 J&F of the dense encoder at ViT-S (-0.12 at ViT-B). Controls qualify the result: protecting only the previous mask under ALGM matches or exceeds accuracy at higher FLOPs (0.486), so expectation and surprise mainly reduce counted encoder compute. Practically, lower counted encoder compute did not reduce startup-inclusive mean Cutie step time on a 12-video LVOS sample (3%-8% lower in steady state; an equally engineered ALGM is faster at ViT-S and ViT-B). Section 5.2 separately examines a Cutie permanent-memory schedule and its latency and memory trade-offs. Code: https://github.com/boletoms/focused-cv

### 22. [DocDUR-Net: Degradation-Aware Unified Document Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34860v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：document image, degradation-aware unified, unified document, image restoration, restoration methods, unified restoration
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34860v1>

**原始摘要**

Captured document images often suffer from diverse degradations caused by uncontrolled imaging conditions, which reduce readability and obscure fine text structures. Single-degradation restoration methods are usually trained and deployed for individual degradation types, and therefore require model selection before restoration. Unified restoration models alleviate the need to maintain multiple restoration networks by sharing one network across tasks, but representative unified document image restoration methods still depend on external task selection. This dependency limits automatic document image restoration. We propose DocDUR-Net, a degradation-aware unified restoration network that derives restoration guidance directly from the input image. It introduces a Degradation Perception Module (DPM) to extract degradation representations and predict degradation weights, and Degradation Prior-guided Adaptive Feature Fusion (DP-AFF) to combine learnable degradation priors with degradation features extracted from the input. The resulting guidance is injected into a shared restoration backbone, enabling adaptive restoration without external task selection. DocDUR-Net also supports a manual mode with explicit task selection. Experiments on document image restoration benchmarks show that DocDUR-Net achieves the best average PSNR of 29.405 dB and SSIM of 0.960 among unified methods while remaining competitive with single-degradation methods.

### 23. [Model Weights, Airlocks and Sandboxes: A Call for Technical Validation of Re-identification Tests under the EDPB Anonymisation Guidelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34859v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：re-identification tests, re-identification attempt MAY MEAN, failed re-identification attempt MAY, CALL for re-identification, re-identification TEST should, ONLY that the TEST
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34859v1>

**原始摘要**

The Guidelines 02/2026 on anonymisation of the European Data Protection Board (EDPB), published for consultation in July 2026, assess anonymity relative to the entities that may access data. They bring AI models into scope and call for testing, but do not say how a re-identification test should be designed or validated, or how far its result counts as evidence. Building on our reply to the consultation, we describe a framework based on a secure processing environment whose outputs are reviewed before release (an airlock), and distinguish forms of release and three types of evidence: formal, empirical and indirect. We raise seven open questions, each with a scenario, and examine their technical side in three settings: the release of model weights (the trained parameters of a machine learning model), review at an airlock, and regulatory sandboxes, in which an authority supervises development and testing. In each setting the assessment depends on how far a test result can be relied on, since a failed re-identification attempt may mean only that the test was too weak. Drawing on measurement practice in other regulated fields, we call for re-identification tests to be validated as measurement methods, with stated detection limit, bias and uncertainty.

### 24. [From Prediction to Decision: Decision-Focused Fine-Tuning of Autoregressive Tabular Transformers for Cost-Sensitive Financial Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34858v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：fine-tuning, autoregressive, tabular transformers for cost-sensitive, cost-sensitive learning whose per-example, transformers for cost-sensitive financial, example-dependent cost-sensitive learning whose
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34858v1>

**原始摘要**

Autoregressive transformers trained on tokenized tabular event streams are a simple and strong baseline for predicting financial events such as fraud. Yet institutions do not act on probabilities; they act on decisions whose consequences are asymmetric and heteroscedastic: the cost of a missed high-value fraud and that of a wrongly declined small purchase are not the same. The standard bridge is post-hoc Bayes-optimal thresholding of calibrated probabilities under a cost matrix. We ask whether fine-tuning the model to the decision objective can do better. We fine-tune the same pretrained model so that its restricted softmax over label tokens (already a distribution over business actions) maximizes expected utility under an institution-specified, amount-scaled cost matrix. Because the reward is a known function of (action, outcome, amount) and the outcome is observed in the data, the expected-utility objective and its gradient are available in closed form. This is example-dependent cost-sensitive learning whose per-example loss is linear in the predicted probability, with no sampling, critics, or advantage estimation. A KL anchor to the pretrained model and an auxiliary language-modeling term preserve calibration and the generative event model. A sampled, group-normalized (GRPO-style) variant provides the extension point to trajectory-level rewards.

### 25. [TIDE-Flow: Fine-Grained Temporal Evidence and Structured Displacement Matching for Spike Optical Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34854v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：fine-grained temporal, displacement evidence, MAY weaken fine-grained firing, RAW spike patterns multi-scale, process MAY weaken fine-grained, spike cameras capture high-speed
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34854v1>

**原始摘要**

Spike cameras capture high-speed motion through an integrate-and-fire mechanism, providing fine-grained temporal observations without exposure blur. However, their binary streams encode accumulated illumination rather than instantaneous intensity, making reliable motion estimation challenging. Existing spike-based methods exploit temporal aggregation to construct motion representations, but this process may weaken fine-grained firing patterns. Meanwhile, correlation-based estimators often rely on learned matching features without explicitly characterizing the spatial distribution and ambiguity of candidate displacements. To address these challenges, we propose TIDE-Flow, a spike-based optical flow estimation framework that jointly exploits Temporal Integration Evidence (TIE) and Displacement Evidence Encoding (DEE). TIE combines raw spike patterns, multi-scale firing statistics, temporal moments, and boundary-aware interval cues, preserving complementary temporal information before spatial feature compression. DEE further integrates structured candidate-grid features with explicit displacement statistics to characterize matching geometry and competing correspondences. Both components are incorporated into a difference-correlation recurrent refinement framework, enabling effective temporal motion modeling and correspondence estimation. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of TIDE-Flow, with controlled ablations validating the contributions of temporal and displacement evidence modeling.

### 26. [Real2SimLab: A Unified Platform for Real-to-Simulation Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34849v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：real2simlab, unified platform for real-to-simulation, platform for real-to-simulation transfer., benchmark, agent, real-to-simulation papers are OPEN
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34849v1>

**原始摘要**

Real-to-simulation transfer, which builds simulations from real-world observations, has long been studied in research and industry. Recently, coding agents have become capable of generating simulation programs from video recordings, and several benchmarks have been proposed to evaluate them. However, each individual benchmark defines its own inputs, deliverables, and scoring pipeline. This leads to $N \times M$ integrations when evaluating $N$ methods on $M$ benchmarks. To this end, we present Real2SimLab, a unified platform for real-to-simulation transfer. It evaluates a method by running its written program and scoring the output simulation with the original scorer of each benchmark. Moreover, Real2SimLab provides a pipeline to create training data and reward annotations for real-to-simulation methods. Our pipeline carefully avoids data contamination by filtering out the video recordings that exist in benchmarks. For each video recording, a program is first produced, and then our pipeline computes its reward with the same code that Real2SimLab uses for evaluation. So far, it supports 47 coding agents and 5 non-agent methods on 6 benchmarks. Both the platform and Awesome-Real2Sim, our research map of nearly 300 real-to-simulation papers, are open source, and we invite the Real2SimHub community to build them together.

### 27. [Put the World Back: Timestamps Matter in Time Series Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34848v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：TIME series, reasoning, alignment, large language models, TIME semantics and event-series, PUT the world BACK
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34848v1>

**原始摘要**

Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly applied to time series reasoning. However, current evaluations often give models events in chronological order and already matched to the time series. This makes it unclear whether models use timestamps to connect events with observations or simply take shortcuts. To expose this ambiguity, we reformulate time series reasoning from a world-to-series projection perspective, viewing timestamps as explicit indices that connect numerical observations and external events to shared world states. We identify three structures that timestamps can make explicit: temporal causal structure, time semantics, and event-series alignment. Building on this perspective, we develop a diagnostic protocol that distinguishes timestamp-grounded reasoning from correlational shortcuts, a distinction that accuracy-based benchmarks cannot make. Controlled interventions on 310 questions from two tasks show that high accuracy can persist when timestamps are replaced by relative offsets or removed, while breaking event-series alignment induces systematic failures. When context is pre-aligned, models barely need timestamps. When order is missing, they use dates to recall world knowledge. Without dates, they rely on correlational shortcuts and rarely notice incorrect timestamps. These findings show that high accuracy on pre-aligned, ordered inputs does not establish that a model uses timestamps to synchronize observations with the world. We release our code, model outputs, and trajectory labels at https://github.com/tingrui-huang/timestamps-matter.

### 28. [Provably Efficient Offline Discounted Reinforcement Learning under Recursive Entropic Risk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34840v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：reinforcement learning, under recursive, recursive entropic, BOTH risk-averse and risk-seeking, agent, finite discounted infinite-horizon markov
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34840v1>

**原始摘要**

We study risk-sensitive reinforcement learning in finite discounted infinite-horizon Markov Decision Processes with recursive entropic risk measures (ERM), parameterized by $β\neq 0$. We consider both risk-averse ($β>0$) and risk-seeking ($β<0$) settings. We consider the offline RL setting, where the agent is provided with a fixed dataset and wishes to learn a near-optimal policy using this dataset. We introduce a model-based algorithm, called ERM Value Iteration Lower Confidence Bound (ERM-VI-LCB) and derive a PAC-type bound on its sample complexity. We also establish a corresponding lower bound, demonstrating that exponential dependence on $|β|/(1-γ)$ asserted by the upper bound is unavoidable in the worst case. The presented bounds provide the first rigorous sample complexity guarantees for offline RL under recursive ERM across both risk-averse and risk-seeking regimes.

### 29. [Diffusion Steering via Adaptive Reward Estimation and Resampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34839v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：alignment, benchmark, diffusion, text-to-image, editing and introduce seam-bench, image editing and introduce
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34839v1>

**原始摘要**

Text-to-image diffusion models can generate images that satisfy complex prompts, yet often produce images that omit requested objects, attributes, or relationships. We ask if models can more reliably generate text-aligned images by redistributing probability toward their more successful outcomes. We propose TrailMix, a training-free method that uses the base model's distribution of possible outcomes to adaptively guide generation toward better text alignment. During denoising, TrailMix generates inexpensive lookahead images from parallel trajectories (trails) and scores their alignment with the prompt using an external judge. The resulting distribution of reward scores allows us to estimate how strongly to steer generation toward a desired alignment level. We use these estimates to reweight and resample trajectories, favoring more promising outcomes. Across three text-to-image benchmarks, TrailMix outperforms existing baselines, with particularly strong gains on rare multi-object compositions. We further extend TrailMix to targeted image editing and introduce SEAM-Bench (Semantic Editing and Attribute Maintenance), a systematic benchmark evaluating whether semantic edits preserve other image content. Experiments on SEAM-Bench and existing benchmark PIE-Bench demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach for controlled image editing. Our results show how a model's own possible outcomes can inform reliable and controllable generation.

### 30. [Proverbs As A Window On Collective Prescriptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34832v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：collective prescriptions, reasoning, large language models, FIND that MOST proverbs, action whereas proverbs about, millennia. YET the collective
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34832v1>

**原始摘要**

Proverbs, as repositories of traditional wisdom, have informed human reasoning for millennia. Yet the collective prescriptions they encode have remained largely unquantified. We introduce a computational framework to analyze nearly 50,000 English-language proverbs. Using large language models, validated against human judgments, we map the topical landscape of proverbs, assess their prescriptive power, and quantify their recommendations on key dimensions of human judgment and behavior. We find that most proverbs are prescriptive, offering guidance in domains under individual control, such as preparation and prudent action, whereas proverbs about uncontrollable circumstances, such as fate and mortality, tend to describe rather than prescribe. When prescriptive proverbs take a consistent stance, they favor caution over risk, long-term over short-term outcomes, honesty over deception, and kindness over malice, and show a moderate tendency toward suspicion over trust. On dimensions involving genuine trade-offs, especially autonomy versus compliance, proverbs provide contradicting advice rather than a single recommendation. This analysis provides a quantitative map of the collective prescriptions in a culture's proverbial wisdom, revealing a core of prudent, honest, and forward-looking guidance alongside varied advice where circumstances dictate the best course of action.

### 31. [Not all world models can doubt: how training objective and architecture shape epistemic uncertainty in JEPA and RSSM world models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34830v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：epistemic uncertainty, world models, shape epistemic, last-layer laplace and ensembles, recurrent state-space model RSSM, uncertainty-penalised planning the RSSM
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34830v1>

**原始摘要**

World models predict how an environment responds to actions, so that candidate actions can be evaluated before execution. Executed actions, or decisions, taken in this manner are only as reliable as the model's predictive capabilities, and predictions in turn are only reliable within the model's experience. Epistemic uncertainty (EU) is the signal that marks where that experience ends. In this work we offer an extensive empirical study on how training objectives and architectures shape epistemic uncertainty of world models. More precisely, we present a controlled comparison of post-hoc EU (last-layer Laplace and ensembles) between two dominant models of different paradigms: 1) LeWorldModel (LeWM): A joint-embedding predictive architecture (JEPA), which predicts the next embedding without a decoder, and 2) DreamerV3: A recurrent state-space model (RSSM), which reconstructs the observation from a recurrent latent state. In ten simulated visual-control environments, we follow a controlled state perturbation of the environment state through every stage of the models up to the final EU estimate. We find that the RSSM attenuates the state perturbation through two mechanisms before it reaches an estimator on the predictor feature. Its recurrent update averages over context, so a change in a single frame reaches the state at a fraction of its size and recovers only as the change persists. Its reconstruction objective weights an entity by the pixels it covers, so a small object can be lost due to the training objective. The JEPA has neither mechanism and passes the change through all stages to the uncertainty. We further show that this translates to gaps in five uncertainty related downstream tasks, from unfamiliar-state detection to uncertainty-penalised planning: the RSSM uncertainty misses deviations that its own posterior registers, whereas the JEPA uncertainty reports what its model sees.

### 32. [Gradient TD($λ$) for Non-Linear Function Approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34826v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：non-linear function, function approximation., reinforcement learning, algorithm for return WAS, value function and present, function and present convergent
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34826v1>

**原始摘要**

Temporal difference (TD) learning is central to reinforcement learning. Many methods use semi-gradient TD, which may diverge, or develop algorithms with mean-squared Bellman or TD-error objectives that are difficult to analyze. Gradient TD algorithms overcome these challenges by using projection of the value function and present convergent off-policy algorithms. However, the algorithm for $λ$-return was limited to linear function approximation. Here, we extend the same to non-linear function approximation, which is desirable since many strong modern function approximators are complex and non-linear. Recent works have derived similar algorithms with a generalized projected Bellman error objective. Alternatively, we continue working with the projected Bellman error.

### 33. [The Linguistic Knowledge Benchmark: Do Language Models Know the Foundations of Early-Grade Literacy?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34823v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：linguistic knowledge, early-grade literacy, knowledge benchmark, TWO BEST models under, kiswahili and the MEAN, KNOW whether THEY HOLD
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34823v1>

**原始摘要**

Many languages of instruction in low- and middle-income countries (LMICs) are poorly represented in the data used to train language models. Before these models can support early-grade literacy in such languages, we need to know whether they hold the linguistic knowledge of each language that literacy teaching relies on. To test this, we present the Linguistic Knowledge Benchmark and the reproducible, largely automated pipeline that turns curriculum documents, teacher guides and pupil books into a vetted question set organised on a fixed grid of nine linguistic domains and seven grade levels. The pipeline combines LLM-driven source survey, feature consolidation, item generation and quality control with an item-selection step inspired by item response theory and a final expert review. We instantiate it for Kiswahili (Kenya; 135 items) and English (United Kingdom; 137 items) and evaluate 121 and 119 models respectively. Frontier models exceed 90% accuracy in both languages, but accuracy falls steeply for smaller and cheaper models, and more so in Kiswahili. The two best models under 10 billion parameters reach 91.7% and 73.0% in English but only 51.9% and 40.7% in Kiswahili, and the mean accuracy of models deployable on mobile and tablet hardware is 47% in English against 24% in Kiswahili. The models most practical for low-resource classrooms are therefore the least capable in the languages they would serve. We release a 20-item sample per language, all pipeline prompts, open-source code, and a public leaderboard. This is the first rung of a three-level evaluation ladder for measuring and generating decodable text to support foundational literacy.

### 34. [LoopTitan: What Does Parameter Revisit Buy a Mixture-of-Experts?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34821v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：parameter revisit, MoE, WHAT DOES parameter, revisit itself, without revisit, BUY using matched non-revisit
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34821v1>

**原始摘要**

Looped Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures revisit layer parameters across depths. This design increases effective depth without adding parameters. Recent looped MoE studies report substantial gains over the standard MoE at matched budgets (parameters, FLOPs and KV cache). However, the reported gains still confound parameter revisit with other design choices, e.g., network shape and residual scale. We ask, what does parameter revisit itself buy? Using matched non-revisit controls, we find that parameter revisit itself buys almost nothing. We argue that revisit only offers a freedom an MoE already has, i.e., trading experts for depth (more layers, fewer experts each) at fixed parameters. First, at matched budgets, we decompose the state-of-the-art looped recipe, with revisit last. A non-revisit control retains all the gain. Most comes from network shape, and the rest from residual scale. We prove this scale acts as a lower learning rate on revisited layers. This helps under AdamW's uneven layer step sizes, but disappears under Muon, which keeps them nearly equal. Second, beyond matched budgets, we evaluate revisit by adding compute at fixed parameters. One extra visit gains more than doubling the experts. Yet without revisit, a deeper standard MoE at equal parameters and compute obtains 90.2% of that gain under Muon. Overall, gains come from network shape, the optimizer and added compute, which a standard MoE can adopt. Accordingly, we propose LoopTitan-Base as a stronger MoE baseline. Under Muon, it matches the strongest looped recipe without revisit or residual scale. We open-source LoopTitan and release 17 pretrained models (including LoopTitan-Base and the control). Our experiment record is public as GitHub issues, with predictions pre-registered and the full exploration history kept. This is our Phase 1 technical report, laying the foundation for follow-up studies.

### 35. [Deep Reinforcement Learning for Multi-UAV Data Harvesting Under Variable Team Sizes and Agent Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34818v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：reinforcement learning, agent, multi-uav data harvesting under, team-size sampling and exposure, costly and fixed-population policies, learning for multi-uav
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34818v1>

**原始摘要**

Collecting data from wireless sensors can be challenging when limited communication infrastructure prevents them from offloading their measurements. In such settings, unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) can exploit their mobility to harvest data locally, while coordinated UAV teams can provide efficient and redundant coverage of remote areas. Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) enables such teams to learn decentralized coordination policies. However, training separate policies for different team and sensor populations is costly, and fixed-population policies may be vulnerable to agent failures. This work proposes F-MADDPG, a flexible variant of Multi-Agent Deep Deterministic Policy Gradient (MADDPG) that supports data harvesting with varying numbers of wireless sensors and UAVs. The framework also accommodates unexpected catastrophic failures that permanently remove agents during a mission. We compare F-MADDPG with a MADDPG baseline that uses multilayer perceptrons (MLPs) and evaluate training strategies based on team-size sampling and exposure to agent terminations. The results show that F-MADDPG learns across the evaluated population sizes, completes missions faster than the baseline on average, benefits from inverse-cardinality team-size sampling compared with uniform sampling, remains effective after permanent agent losses, and gains no consistent benefit from exposure to failures during training.

### 36. [Transferring Architecture as an Optimization Prior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34816v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：optimization prior, alignment, prior and ASK whether, deeper networks and transferring, determines the optimization FATE, domains instilling the optimization
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34816v1>

**原始摘要**

Initialization determines the optimization "fate" of a model, going far beyond just setting the scale of its weights. It establishes a fundamental initial geometry over the input data, dictating certain aspects such as which examples are considered close and which representations gradient descent will naturally refine first. In this paper, we view the initial geometry of a network as an optimization prior and ask whether a network can change its optimization prior by referencing a completely different architecture. To investigate this question, we use an initialization procedure where a target network is aligned to the representational geometry of a randomly initialized guide network before any downstream training occurs. Crucially, alignment to the guide network cannot transfer learned features, never receives gradients, and does not use labels. We demonstrate that this cross-architecture transfer of "fate" benefits the target network in two key domains: (1) instilling the optimization benefits of depth into shallow networks by aligning them to deeper networks, and (2) transferring representational granularity by using fine-resolution networks to shape the geometry of networks with coarser representations. In both cases, the alignment results in improved downstream performance of the target network. Ultimately, this demonstrates that a model's architectural destiny can be decoupled from its physical structure and explicitly modified at initialization.

### 37. [Solve Vision with Code: Coding Agents Are Stronger Visual Reasoners Than Video Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34813v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：visual reasoning, video generation, agent, THAN video, rather THAN, visual reasoners
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34813v1>

**原始摘要**

Video generation models are increasingly evaluated as visual reasoners: given a first frame and a natural-language prompt, can the model produce the video in which the specified task is carried out? The paradigm is considered advantageous because the reasoning is native to the visual medium, unfolding directly in the frames rather than through language or code. Whether this native route is in fact the more reliable path to visual reasoning has not been tested against a symbolic alternative under the same conditions. We therefore pose the same tasks, with identical inputs, output specification and evaluator, to both media: rather than denoising pixels, a coding agent writes a program that renders the answer video, which an unmodified rule-based evaluator scores. On VBVR-Pro-Bench (Xu et al., 2026), a 500-instance suite spanning five cognitive categories with symbolic ground truth, the agent receives the same first frame and prompt as the video model, with no access to ground truth, metadata or generation models, and one attempt per instance. Three closed-model agents (Codex with gpt-6-astra, Claude Code with Fable 5.1 and Gemini CLI with Gemini 3.1 Pro) score 0.923, 0.894 and 0.893, each more than 0.22 above the strongest video model. Among 23 runnable open-weight models, the best, GLM-5, reaches 0.572, above all video models except the RL-trained one. Together, we show that for tasks whose solutions can be computed from a first frame and a prompt, program synthesis is currently a substantially more reliable route to visual reasoning than video generation. We further distinguish depictive programs, which navigate the image to recover a transferable scheme, from propositional programs, which operate on hard-coded values, revealing how visual reasoning unfolds in agent-written programs and what it implies for its nature of reasoning in general.

### 38. [HOG-WAM: Improving Visual Robustness through Future Gradient Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34811v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：attention, diffusion, transformer, hog-wam which ADDS future, hog-wam achieves the highest, introduce hog-wam which ADDS
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34811v1>

**原始摘要**

World-action models jointly predict future observations and actions, yet visual corruption can disrupt the resulting policies. We introduce HOG-WAM, which adds future Histograms of Oriented Gradients (HOG) as structural supervision derived directly from demonstration images. An auxiliary HOG diffusion transformer (DiT) module reads RGB features through asymmetric attention, allowing its objective to shape the visual representation used for control. The module is used only during training and removed at inference, preserving FastWAM's RGB-to-action architecture. We evaluate four controlled observation corruptions through a visual-corruption extension of RoboTwin, termed RoboTwin-Plus; HOG-WAM achieves the highest success among the compared policies under all four. Mixed-noise success increases from 78.86% to 82.86% relative to FastWAM, with comparable performance on clean scenes. On LIBERO Plus, overall success increases from 74.49% to 75.26%, with the largest gain under sensor noise, from 74.14% to 78.89%. Across four Franka tasks, average success increases from 40% to 59% under clean observations and from 17% to 24% under noisy observations. A representation ablation favors combining RGB observations with auxiliary HOG prediction over replacing RGB with HOG. These findings support future-gradient prediction as complementary supervision for visually robust control, with larger absolute gains on physical tasks than in simulation. Code is available at https://github.com/iPPLab/HOG-WAM.

### 39. [Split-RL: Local Conflict Resolution in Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34799v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：reinforcement learning, WHEN the split-rl score, reach the leaves. split-rl, split-rl leverages this BIAS, characterize WHEN the split-rl, split-rl localizes the tradeoff
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34799v1>

**原始摘要**

Reinforcement learning (RL) policies are often trained by mixing contradictory feedback that is localized in space and time. For example, a robot may prioritize speed in open areas versus precision in tight gaps. We introduce Split-RL, a Gradient Boosting Trees (GBT) algorithm that uses guidance labels to locally isolate these conflicting signals. Standard neural networks (NNs) lack the structural mechanism to do this, as their parameters share feedback from all sources. While GBT provides the inductive bias to explicitly partition the state-space per objective, standard tree-fitting procedures aggregate contradictory gradient signals before they reach the leaves. Split-RL leverages this bias to integrate guidance labels into tree construction, routing these updates into disjoint state-space regions and preventing signal interference. Such labels can arise from heuristics, sensor-derived events, or existing rule-based systems, allowing Split-RL to incorporate rule-derived structure while retaining a learned policy. We theoretically show how early gradient aggregation loses objective-specific information and characterize when the Split-RL score isolates conflicting updates. We focus on constrained RL and offline RL with mixed datasets, each balancing two objectives that conflict locally. Across domains with spatial, rare-event, and temporal conflicts, Split-RL consistently outperforms existing methods by meeting the episodic cost budget while maintaining competitive rewards. In offline settings, Split-RL localizes the tradeoff between imitation and policy improvement, outperforming other methods and achieving near-expert performance across varying dataset sizes.

### 40. [PersonaFactory: Stylistic User Prompt Augmentation with Synthetic Persona Bank](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34785v1)

- 来源：arXiv
- 关键词：USER prompts, alignment, large language models, fidelity THAN free-text persona, THAN free-text persona descriptions, roughly million persona-styled synthetic
- 链接：<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34785v1>

**原始摘要**

Large language models increasingly replace real people in the AI development pipeline, as they are used to generate training dialogues at scale and to drive alignment and evaluation. However, out-of-the-box models produce monolingual and stylistically flat texts as synthetic user prompts, failing to replicate the real user-AI communication mechanics. We present PersonaFactory, an end-to-end pipeline that generates detailed user personas and uses them to augment existing user prompts from their point of view, producing a bank of over 740000 structured synthetic personas detailing their background and communication behaviors, and a corpus of roughly 1.24 million persona-styled synthetic conversations spanning 85 languages. We evaluate the effect of personal features on style adoption and intent preservation, using an LLM-judge and heuristic metrics to assess augmentation fidelity together with the downstream impact on assistant persona control through training data.
